QUESTION: Under what conditions may the insurer contest this policy?
CATEGORY: CAT3_CONDITIONAL_REASONING
EXPECTED ANSWER: The insurer may contest only within the first two years while the insured is living, and only for material misrepresentation in the application.

--- GOAL PLAN ---
rewritten_query: What are the general conditions under which an insurer may contest an insurance policy?
reasoning: The query asks about the conditions under which an insurer may contest a specific insurance policy. This is a legal/contractual question that depends on the policy's terms, applicable laws, and standard insurance practices. Since the query does not specify a particular policy, insurer, or jurisdiction, it is asking for general conditions under which contestability applies—commonly found in life insurance policies. The key concept here is "contestability period," typically a two-year window after policy issuance during which insurers can contest claims based on misrepresentation or fraud in the application. This is a well-defined, standard concept in insurance law. The query is not multi-hop—it seeks a single, definitional fact about policy contestability conditions. Therefore, only one sub-goal is needed.
